The conflict intensified after a series of arguments about her weekend boxing schedule and church commitments, and she increasingly poured herself into extra shifts and community activities rather than addressing problems at home. Over approximately ten months, her family and church friends observed that she became markedly less reliable—forgetting potlucks she’d organized, showing up to boxing and work in an unusually agitated, driven state, and reacting defensively when others questioned her decisions—leading to friction with coworkers and threats that she might lose her job. A particularly chaotic evening, when she left home after a heated argument and her husband had to call her sister and pastor to help calm the situation, led the family to bring her to the emergency department, where psychiatric services were consulted.\n- Chief Complaint: “I’ve been feeling wound up and on edge for months, like my mood is stuck on high and I’m snapping at everyone, even my own family.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I know it sounds crazy to other people, but I’m sure the folks at the warehouse and even some people at church are watching me and trying to set me up to get fired, like they’re all in on some plan because I’m meant to do something big and they’re scared of that.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Pretty much every day now, especially at night or when I’m alone in the break room, I hear these voices clear as day arguing about me, saying I’m a disappointment or that I need to ‘step up and save people,’ even when nobody’s around and there’s no radio on.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s going on, my words just run together and I jump from talking about work to church to boxing to my husband, and people keep stopping me saying they can’t follow what I’m saying, even though in my head it all connects.\n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): Sometimes at work or at home I get so wound up and confused that I just pace back and forth for an hour, talking to myself, flapping my hands or making these weird little gestures, and if someone tells me to sit down or calm down I either ignore them or snap at them without really meaning to.\n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Other times, especially after one of those big blowups, I just shut down for days, barely talk to anyone, skip boxing and church, sit on the couch staring at the TV without really watching it, and even taking a shower or cooking feels like too much effort.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): For months I’ve felt like I’m stuck on high voltage, bouncing between feeling super amped up and invincible to getting pissed off at the smallest thing, like my husband asking about the bills or a coworker moving my pallet, and I end up yelling or slamming stuff before I even realize I’m doing it.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): It’s like I’ve got a motor inside me that won’t shut off—I’m cleaning the house at 3 a.m., reorganizing the stock room at work when I’m supposed to be loading, signing up for extra shifts and church events, and I can’t sit still long enough to finish any one thing.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): Most nights I only sleep two or three hours, if that, because my mind is racing with ideas and plans, and instead of feeling tired I just get up and start doing things like writing lists, cleaning, or texting people in the middle of the night.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll be in the middle of a serious talk with my boss or my husband and suddenly I’ll get pulled off by a buzzing light, a sound in the hallway, or a random thought about a new project, and next thing I know I’m off doing something else and never finished what I started.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I’ve been signing up for extra overtime, trying to start little side hustles, planning new church groups, and even pushing my husband to have sex way more than usual all at the same time, like I have to chase every idea right then or I’ll explode.\n- S041 (Painful_Consequence_Behavior): I’ve blown a bunch of money we don’t really have on boxing gear, church donations, and stuff online, driven way too fast when I’m upset, and met up with a couple guys from the gym behind my husband’s back because in the moment it all felt exciting and right and I didn’t really care what might happen after.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently: Back when I first started feeling super amped up and barely sleeping, that was also when the voices really kicked in and I became convinced people at work and church were plotting against me, all happening at the same time for months.\n- manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness: For most of this whole stretch I’ve felt revved up, overactive, and wired, with only short spells where I crash and go quiet before I ramp right back up again.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode: There was a period a couple months ago when my mood felt more flat and drained than amped up, but the voices and the feeling that everyone was watching and testing me never let up during those weeks.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve been written up at the warehouse for not following instructions and leaving tasks half-done, my husband has talked seriously about separating, and I’ve stopped showing up reliably to church and boxing even though those used to be my main supports.\n- duration: Looking back, this whole thing has been building and messing with my life for close to a year now, pretty much since the money fights with my husband really started.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: I’ve been hearing the voices and feeling like people are out to get me for most of this time, definitely going strong for at least the last eight months.\n- manic_episode_duration: Feeling wired, barely sleeping, and constantly on the go has been happening for around half a year now, ever since I first started grabbing all those extra shifts at the too fast when I’m upset, and met up with a couple guys from the gym behind my husband’s back because in the moment it all felt exciting and right and I didn’t really care what might happen after.\n▸ warehouse for not following instructions and leaving tasks half-done, my husband has talked seriously about separating, and I’ve stopped showing up reliably to church and boxing even though those used to be my main supports.\n- duration: Looking back, this whole thing has been building and messing with my life for close to a year now, pretty much since the money fights with my husband really It was like those things were running on a completely separate track from my mood.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of at least two weeks where you experienced these voices and feelings of being watched when you were NOT experiencing significant mood symptoms like being 'wired' or 'flat'?\n\nPatient: Yes, a couple of months ago there was a period of a few weeks where my mood wasn't feeling super amped up or wired. Even during those weeks, the voices and the feeling of being watched and tested never let up.\n\nDoctor: During those weeks when your mood was stable, were the voices and the feeling of being watched your only major symptoms, or did you also experience other changes in your thinking, such as disorganized speech or a significant lack of motivation and social withdrawal?"

Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation. Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
